logic\n\n==========================================\nWHY EACH CHANGE MATTERS\n==========================================\n\nSLOT_HEIGHT 4.5 -> 6.0:\n  Butt is 3.64mm tall.\n  At V6.1: 4.5 - 3.64 = 0.86mm total clearance (0.43mm/side)\n  PETG print tolerance: ~0.2mm\n  Worst case clearance: 0.46mm - tight, especially at transitions\n  \n  At V6.2: 6.0 - 3.64 = 2.36mm clearance (1.18mm/side)\n  Worst case: 0.98mm - comfortable, robust to print variation\n\nPROFILE_STEPS 360 -> 720:\n  Slope: 10mm lift / 22.5deg rise = 0.444mm per degree\n  At 1deg steps: each segment is 0.44mm taller than previous\n  At 0.2mm PETG layer height, each step spans 2+ layers = visible stairs\n  \n  At 0.5deg steps: each segment is 0.22mm taller\n  Now matches PETG layer height = slicer blends smoothly\n  No staircase artifacts on cam surface\n\nRise 22.5 -> 30deg (gentler slope):\n  Steeper rise = faster needle motion = more stress on butt-cam contact\n  Gentler rise = smoother operation, less wear, less vibration\n  Slope: 0.444 -> 0.333 mm/deg (25% gentler)\n  \n  Real CSMs: typical rise is 25-35deg\n  V6.1 (22.5) was on aggressive end\n  V6.2 (30) is more typical\n\nZ_OFFSET tuning param:\n  If first print needs Z shift (e.g. cam too high or too low),\n  just change Z_OFFSET, regenerate, reprint\n  Don't need to touch the profile logic\n\n==========================================\nDIMENSIONS (V6.2)\n==========================================\n\nRing:\n  OD:               165mm\n  ID:               115mm (clears 114.3mm Slot height = 6mm\nThis was necessary. Your previous 4.5mm would almost certainly bind in real life.\nNow you have:\n\n* Butt \u2248 3.6mm\n* Clearance \u2248 1.2mm per side\n\ud83d\udc49 That\u2019s safe for:\n\n* PETG deformation\n* slight misalignment\n* real-world dirt + fuzz\n\u2705 4. 0.5\u00b0 segmentation\nSmart move. No entry/exit blending (this is subtle but critical)\nRight now:\n\n* At 270\u00b0 \u2192 instant slope start\n* At 300\u00b0 \u2192 instant slope end\nEven though it's linear, the derivative is discontinuous.\nThat creates:\n\n* jerk (in physics terms)\n* latch shock\n* inconsistent yarn pickup\n\ud83d\udd27 Fix (important)\nAdd a small easing function:\nInstead of linear:\nf(x)=xf(x) = xf(x)=x\nUse something like:\nf(x)=3x2\u22122x3f(x) = 3x^2 - 2x^3f(x)=3x2\u22122x3\nThis gives:\n\n* zero slope at start/end\n* smoother needle motion\n\u274c 3. 10mm lift may be too aggressive (for first build)\nYou\u2019re assuming:\n\n* full clearing\n* ideal needle geometry\nBut in practice:\n\n* too much lift = latch instability\n* yarn miss timing\n* unnecessary stress\n\ud83d\udd27 Safer starting point:\n\n* 8mm lift\n* keep Z_OFFSET for tuning\n\ud83d\udc49 Then increase if needed.\n\u274c 4. No friction strategy (this will bite you fast)\nYou\u2019re printing in PETG and running:\n\n* steel needle butts\n* sliding contact\n* repeated cycles\nThat means:\n\n* wear grooves quickly\n* inconsistent motion after short use\n\ud83d\udd27 Minimum fix:\n\n* polish cam track (sand + PTFE spray)\n\ud83d\udd27 Better:\n\n* print in Nylon (PA6/PA12) or\n* insert a metal cam liner\n\u274c 5. Discontinuous derivative at start (0 -> 0.27 mm/deg instantly)\n  - Discontinuous derivative at end (0.27 -> 0 instantly)\n  - Result: infinite \"jerk\" on needle = latch shock, vibration\n\nSMOOTHSTEP easing f(x) = 3x^2 - 2x^3:\n  - Slope at start (x=0): 0 mm/deg\n  - Slope at middle (x=0.5): 0.4 mm/deg (50% higher than linear avg)\n  - Slope at end (x=1): 0 mm/deg\n  - Result: jerk = 0 at endpoints = smooth latch operation\n\nTrade-off: peak slope 50% higher in middle of motion\n  But finite jerk vs infinite jerk = much smoother actual operation\n\nThis is standard Sand cam track surface with 400-grit sandpaper\n  2. Then 1000-grit sandpaper for finish\n  3. Wipe with isopropyl alcohol\n  4. Apply PTFE lubricant before assembly\n\n==========================================\nTEST SLICE STRATEGY\n==========================================\n\nDO NOT print full ring before validating with test slice.\n\nTest slice covers 75 degrees (260-335deg):\n  - 10deg of REST before rise\n  - 30deg of RISE (smoothstep)\n  - 5deg of PEAK\n  - 20deg of FALL (smoothstep)\n  - 10deg of REST after fall\n\nIf it works on test slice, it will work on full ring.\nTest slice cost: ~1.5 hours, ~35g PETG\nFull ring cost: ~6 hours, ~190g PETG\n\n==========================================\nTEST PROTOCOL\n==========================================\n\nStep 1: Print test slice\nStep 2: Sand cam track (400 then 1000 grit)\nStep 3: Apply PTFE lubricant\nStep 4: Mount near cylinder (with needles installed)\nStep 5: Slowly rotate cylinder, watch needle motion\nStep 6: Measure max lift with caliper (should be 8mm +/- 0.5mm)\nStep 7: Verify smooth motion through rise/peak/fall zones\nStep 8: Decision:\n  PASS -> print full ring\n  FAIL (lift wrong) -> adjust LIFT or Z_OFFSET, reprint slice\n  FAIL (binding) -> increase SLOT_HEIGHT, reprint slice\n\n==========================================\nHARDWARE NEEDED\n==========================================\n\nAlready have:\n  - PETG filament (DRY!)\n  - 6x M5 bolts (B0FFSNFK3Y)\n  - 6x 4mm dowel pins (B0FDWVGMQ9 / B0F5HFFVP7)\n  - Loctite 603 (B0074NALBO)\n  - PTFE lubricant (B003UTX0R8)\n  - Sandpaper assortment (B07DTGP1QT)\n\nNothing new to order.\n\n==========================================\nASSEMBLY (after test slice validates)\n==========================================\n\n1. At 1440 with 2\u00d7 boolean operations, time grows non-linearly \u2014 could be hours per macro run. The 0.22mm step is below the noise floor of PETG print tolerance + flex + lubricant film. **Defer to V7 if real-world wear becomes a problem.**\n\n## What I Verified Mathematically\n\nI numerically tested the smoothstep cam profile:\n\n```\ntheta=270 (rise start):  z=5.000, slope=0.026 mm/deg  \u2190 near-zero (good!)\ntheta=285 (rise mid):    z=9.000, slope=0.400 mm/deg  \u2190 peak (50% above linear)\ntheta=299.5 (rise end):  z=12.97, slope=0.026 mm/deg  \u2190 near-zero (good!)\ntheta=300 (peak start):  z=13.000\ntheta=302 (peak):        z=13.000\ntheta=305 (fall start):  z=13.000\ntheta=315 (fall mid):    z=9.000\ntheta=325 (fall end):    z=5.000\n```\n\nThe smoothstep produces **near-zero slope at endpoints** (cam motion starts and ends gently) but **higher peak slope in the middle**. This is the textbook trade-off for jerk-free cam motion.\n\n## V6.3 Locked Geometry\n\n| Parameter | Value | Change |\n|---|---|---|\n| Ring OD | 165mm | unchanged |\n| Ring ID | 115mm | unchanged |\n| Ring height | 35mm | unchanged |\n| Slot height | 6.0mm | unchanged from V6.2 |\n| Slot depth | 3.0mm | unchanged |\n| Z rest | 5mm | unchanged |\n| **Z peak** | **13mm** | V6.3 (was 15) due to LIFT change |\n| Z offset | 0.0mm | unchanged (tuning) |\n| **LIFT** | **8mm** | **V6.3** (was 10) \u2014 conservative |\n| Profile steps | 720 | unchanged |\n| **Easing** | **Smoothstep** | **V6.3** (was linear) |\n| Knit zone | 55\u00b0 | unchanged |\n| Avg slope | 0.267 mm/deg | from 8mm/30deg |\n| Peak slope | 0.400 mm/deg | smoothstep midpoint |\n| Endpoint slope | 0.000 mm/deg | jerk = 0 |\n\n## Friction Strategy (Documented in README)\n\nFor V1.0 build:\n1. Slope transitions (smoothstep deceleration zones)\n    - Friction transients (PETG + PTFE not perfectly uniform)\n    - Misalignment (slight cam tilt)\n  Result: micro-impacts, vibration, inconsistent stitch timing\n  \n  With top lip, butt is constrained to follow bottom cam more tightly\n  in the active zone where motion happens, but stays loose elsewhere\n  where binding would be a concern.\n\nSafety check:\n  Earlier reviewer (V6.1) said 0.43mm/side clearance = binding\n  V6.4 in knit zone: 0.78mm/side - SAFELY ABOVE binding threshold\n  PETG print tolerance \u00b10.2mm: worst case 0.58mm - still safe\n\n==========================================\nDESIGN PHILOSOPHY (CONFLICTING REVIEWS RESOLVED)\n==========================================\n\nTwo reviews flagged opposite concerns:\n  Review A (V6.1->V6.2): \"Slot too tight = binding\"\n  Review B (V6.3->V6.4): \"Slot too loose = jump risk\"\n\nInitially I dismissed Review B as \"contradicting\" Review A.\nThat was wrong reasoning.\n\nThese are two ENDS of a tolerance window:\n  Tight: binding\n  Just right: smooth operation\n  Loose: jump/contact loss\n\nV6.4 acknowledges both with the elegant compromise:\n  - Outside knit zone: loose (1.18mm/side) - tolerant of print error\n  - Inside knit zone: controlled (0.78mm/side) - prevents jump\n  - Both ranges safely above binding threshold (0.4mm/side)\n\nThis \"local constraint\" approach gives ~80% of full top cam benefit\nat ~5% of the complexity.\n\n==========================================\nCHANGES FROM V6.3 (KEPT)\n==========================================\n\n  - SMOOTHSTEP cubic easing (jerk-free at endpoints)\n  - LIFT 8mm (conservative for first build)\n  - SLOT_HEIGHT 6.0mm (full clearance outside knit zone)\n  - 720 angular segments (0.5deg each)\n  - Z_OFFSET tuning parameter\n\n==========================================\nDIMENSIONS (V6.4)\n==========================================\n\nRing:\n  OD:               165mm\n  ID:               115mm (clears 114.3mm cylinder)\n  Height:           35mm\n  Wall thickness:   25mm\n\nCam slot:\n  Slot height:      6.0mm axial (outside knit zone)\n                    5.2mm axial (inside knit zone, with top lip)\n  Slot depth:       3.0mm radial\n  Z rest (bottom):  5mm\n  Z peak (bottom):  13mm\n  Lift amount:      8mm\n  Z offset:         0.0mm (tuning)\n\nTop lip (V6.4 NEW):\n  Height:           0.8mm\n  Zone start:       270deg\n  Zone end:         325deg\n  Coverage:         55deg (matches knit zone exactly)\n\nCam profile (smoothstep):\n  Rest zone:    0-270deg + 325-360deg = 305deg total\n  Rise zone:    270-300deg = 30deg (smoothstep)\n  Peak zone:    300-305deg = 5deg\n  Fall zone:    305-325deg = 20deg (smoothstep)\n  Total knit zone: 55deg\n  Average slope: 0.267 mm/deg\n  Endpoint slope: 0.000 mm/deg (jerk-free)\n\nMounting (unchanged):\n  6x M5 bolts at PCD 155mm\n  6x 4mm pins at PCD 145mm (1 round + 5 slotted)\n\n==========================================\nPRINT INSTRUCTIONS (UNCHANGED FROM V6.3)\n==========================================\n\nMaterial:       PETG (DRY filament essential)\nLayer height:   0.16mm (NOT 0.2mm) - smoother cam surface\nPerimeters:     5\nTop/bottom:     6 layers\nInfill:         40-50% Cubic\nOrientation:    ring lying flat (axis Z up)\nSupports:       NONE\nOuter wall speed: 20-25 mm/s\nCooling fan:    100% after layer 3\n\nTime estimates:\n  Test slice (75deg): ~1-1.5 hours, ~35g PETG\n  Full ring:          ~5-7 hours, ~180-200g PETG\n\nPOST-PRINT FINISHING:\n  1. Sand cam track bottom AND top lip surfaces (400-grit then 1000-grit)\n  2. Wipe with isopropyl alcohol\n  3. Apply PTFE lubricant\n  4. Mount and test\n\n==========================================\nTEST PROTOCOL\n==========================================\n\nStep 1: Print test slice (75deg covers full knit zone with top lip)\nStep 2: Inspect printed top lip - should be ~0.8mm intrusion in knit zone\nStep 3: Sand both surfaces (cam bottom AND top lip)\nStep 4: Apply PTFE lubricant\nStep 5: Mount near cylinder with needle installed\nStep 6: Slowly rotate, watch motion\nStep 7: Check:\n  - Lift amount (~8mm)\n  - Smooth motion (no binding from top lip)\n  - No \"jump\" at slope transitions\n  - Butt stays in slot throughout\n\nIf TOO TIGHT (binding):\n  Reduce TOP_LIP_HEIGHT to 0.5mm\n  Reprint slice\n\nIf JUMP STILL OCCURS:\n  Increase TOP_LIP_HEIGHT to 1.0mm\n  Reprint slice\n\n==========================================\nHARDWARE NEEDED\n==========================================\n\nAlready have:\n  - PETG filament (DRY!)\n  - 6x M5 bolts (B0FFSNFK3Y)\n  - 6x 4mm dowel pins (B0FDWVGMQ9 / B0F5HFFVP7)\n  - Loctite 603 (B0074NALBO)\n  - PTFE lubricant (B003UTX0R8)\n  - Sandpaper assortment (B07DTGP1QT)\n\nNothing new to order.\n\n==========================================\nNEXT STEPS AFTER CAM RING\n==========================================\n\nPhase complete: Cam Ring V6.4\nNext: Sinker Ring V1.0\nThen: Retainer Ring V1.0 (PA12)\nThen: Cassette Base V1.0 (200mm OD, 6 feeder mounts)\nThen: Feeder Module V1.0 (mechanical + servo)\nThen: Yarn Mast V1.0\nEOF\n\ncd csm_camring_v64\nzip -r /mnt/user-data/outputs/CSM_V3_CamRing_V6_4_DELIVERY.zip 3D-Project -q\n\necho \"=== ZIP CONTENTS ===\"\nunzip -l /mnt/user-data/outputs/CSM_V3_CamRing_V6_4_DELIVERY.zip\necho \"\"\nls -lh /mnt/user-data/outputs/CSM_V3_CamRing_V6_4_DELIVERY.zip",

If the butt is sitting high in the slot at those moments, it could slam into the suddenly-descended ceiling. **This needs smoothstep blending.**\n\n## What I'm Implementing in V6.5\n\n- **Keep:** \"shorter ceiling\" approach (V6.4 implementation)\n- **Add:** smoothstep blending of TOP_LIP_HEIGHT at zone boundaries (over ~3\u00b0)\n- **Document:** \"true overhang\" as V7+ option if jump issues persist after testing\n\nLet me build V6.5:",

Secondary impact at zone boundary\n    - Audible \"click\" during operation\n    - Inconsistent transition behavior\n  \n  Smooth blending eliminates this by:\n    - Lip = 0 at theta = 270 (start of knit zone)\n    - Lip ramps up to 0.8mm by theta = 273 (3deg blend)\n    - Lip = 0.8mm full from 273 to 322\n    - Lip ramps back down to 0 over 322-325\n    - Lip = 0 at theta >= 325 (end of knit zone)\n\n==========================================\nWHY I DIDN'T IMPLEMENT TRUE OVERHANG (HONEST DISCLOSURE)\n==========================================\n\nThe reviewer also suggested implementing a \"real lip\" with radial\noverhang that projects inward from the top corner of the slot.\n\nI CHOSE not to implement this because:\n\n1. Functional equivalence for our geometry:\n   The needle butt is anchored to a rotating cylinder. It cannot\n   move radially outward beyond the cylinder OD. So the only motion\n   we need to constrain is vertical (Z). Both my \"shorter ceiling\"\n   and a \"true overhang\" constrain Z motion at the same height.\n   The radial-overhang advantage applies to designs where the butt\n   could escape radially - not ours.\n\n2. Implementation simplicity:\n   Shorter ceiling = simple cylindrical cut, easy to print.\n   True overhang = complex geometry with concave feature, harder to\n   print on FDM (would need supports or fail).\n\n3. Recovery path:\n   If post-test results show the shorter ceiling isn't enough,\n   V7 can implement true overhang. Easy to swap.\n\nFor first build: V6.5 is sufficient.\nIf wear/jump issues arise after testing: V7 with true overhang.\n\n==========================================\nCHANGES FROM V6.4 (KEPT)\n==========================================\n\n  - Z_OFFSET tuning parameter\n\n==========================================\nDIMENSIONS (V6.5)\n==========================================\n\nRing:\n  OD:               165mm\n  ID:               115mm\n  Height:           35mm\n  Wall thickness:   25mm\n\nCam slot:\n  Height (outside knit): 6.0mm\n  Height (full knit):    5.2mm (with 0.8mm lip)\n  Slot depth:            3.0mm radial\n  Z rest (bottom):       5mm\n  Z peak (bottom):       13mm\n  Lift amount:           8mm\n  Z offset:              0.0mm (tuning)\n\nTop lip (V6.5):\n  Max height:            0.8mm\n  Zone start:            270deg\n  Zone end:              325deg\n  Blend angle:           3deg (V6.5 NEW)\n  Behavior:              Smoothstep ramp in/out\n\nCam profile (smoothstep):\n  Rest zone:    0-270deg + 325-360deg = 305deg total\n  Rise zone:    270-300deg = 30deg (smoothstep)\n  Peak zone:    300-305deg = 5deg\n  Fall zone:    305-325deg = 20deg (smoothstep)\n  Total knit zone: 55deg\n\nMounting (unchanged):\n  6x M5 bolts at PCD 155mm\n  6x 4mm pins at PCD 145mm\n\n==========================================\nPRINT INSTRUCTIONS (UNCHANGED FROM V6.3)\n==========================================\n\nMaterial:       PETG (DRY)\nLayer height:   0.16mm (NOT 0.2mm)\nPerimeters:     5\nTop/bottom:     6 layers\nInfill:         40-50% Cubic\nOrientation:    ring lying flat (axis Z up)\nSupports:       NONE\nOuter wall speed: 20-25 mm/s\nCooling fan:    100% after layer 3\n\nTime:\n  Test slice: ~1-1.5 hours, ~35g PETG\n  Full ring:  ~5-7 hours, ~180-200g PETG\n\nPOST-PRINT FINISHING:\n  1. Here's what changed:\n\n### Key Updates\n\n| Item | Old | New | Reason |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **Servo type** | 4-pack SG90 plastic | **8\u00d7 MG90S metal** | Eliminate 50-100 hour failure window |\n| **Servo cost** | $13 | **$160** | Metal gears = 800+ hour life |\n| **Spares included** | 2 (from 4-pack) | **2** (strategic) | Insurance for defects or experiments |\n| **Total project cost** | $1,328 | **$1,468** | ~$140 premium justified by reliability |\n| **Total expanded (6-feeder)** | ~$1,400 | **~$1,510** | No future servo purchases needed |\n\n### Why This Wins\n\n**SG90 failure lifecycle:**\n- Plastic gears die at 50-100 hours (right in your testing window)\n- Costs you time diagnosing + 3-4 hours replacing mid-knit\n- Replacement cost $13 + your frustration\n\n**MG90S lifecycle:**\n- Metal gears last 800+ hours (never during prototype phase)\n- 2 spares in inventory ($40) = insurance for manufacturing defects\n- Total lifecycle cost is actually **cheaper** when factoring in replacement labor\n\n### What the BOM Now Says\n\n**Servo section explains:**\n> \"SG90 plastic gears fail at 50-100 hours (mid-testing). MG90S metal gears last 800+ hours (never during prototype phase). Cost of failure: 3-4 hours diagnosis + replacement + frustration. Not worth $70 savings.\"\n\n**Future expansion simplified:**\n- No more \"buy another 4-pack SG90 later\"\n- You already have 8 servos: use 6 for F1-F6, keep 2 spares\n- Only future purchases are yarn cones + yarn mast hardware\n\n### Budget Impact\n\n- **Budget:** $1,230-1,500\n- **Project:** $1,468 (slightly over, but justified)\n- **Reason:** Metal-gear reliability is critical for testing\n\nThis is the right call. You're not saving $70 \u2014 you're investing $140 to **avoid a catastrophic failure at 80 hours when the system is finally working.**\n\n---\n\n**Next steps:** Do you have other thoughts to incorporate into the BOM, or shall we move on to **Sinker Ring V1.0** design?",
